class="markdown-body" data-turbolinks="false"><div class="markdown-body safe-html-box__Uwc6Al Container__ZAxOBo"><p dir="auto"><strong>Current behavior</strong></p>
<p dir="auto">I am using inside my application</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-xml notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="        &lt;dependency&gt;
            &lt;groupId&gt;io.quarkiverse.cxf&lt;/groupId&gt;
            &lt;artifactId&gt;quarkus-cxf-integration-tracing-opentelemetry&lt;/artifactId&gt;
        &lt;/dependency&gt;"><pre class="notranslate">        &lt;<span class="pl-ent">dependency</span>&gt;
            &lt;<span class="pl-ent">groupId</span>&gt;io.quarkiverse.cxf&lt;/<span class="pl-ent">groupId</span>&gt;

        &lt;/<span class="pl-ent">dependency</span>&gt;</pre></div>
<p dir="auto">to push my traces inside our APM.</p>
<p dir="auto">I am using <code class="notranslate">io.quarkus:quarkus-jdbc-oracle</code> and <code class="notranslate">io.quarkus:quarkus-hibernate-orm</code></p>
<p dir="auto">When build in native mode it fails regarding the usage of <strong>com.sun.jmx.mbeanserver.JmxMBeanServer</strong></p>
<p dir="auto"><strong>Expected behavior</strong></p>
<p dir="auto">The native build should be ok and produce a native executable.</p>
<p dir="auto"><strong>How to reproduce</strong></p>
<ol dir="auto">
<li>git clone quarkus-cxf</li>
<li>checkout commit "[maven-release-plugin] prepare release 3.18.0"</li>
<li>in integration-tests/opentelemetry update pom.xml by adding in maven dependencies:</li>
</ol>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-xml notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="        &lt;dependency&gt;

<p dir="auto">I do not think we can do anything about this in Quarkus CXF. From my point of view the GraalVM points to analysis shout take the Quarkus Substitution for oracle.jdbc.driver.OracleDriver into account:</p>
<p dir="auto"><a href="https://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/f886e7bd7e061c52d78db014e5e97da15be26a64/extensions/jdbc/jdbc-oracle/runtime/src/main/java/io/quarkus/jdbc/oracle/runtime/graal/OracleDriverSubstitutions.java">https://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/f886e7bd7e061c52d78db014e5e97da15be26a64/extensions/jdbc/jdbc-oracle/runtime/src/main/java/io/quarkus/jdbc/oracle/runtime/graal/OracleDriverSubstitutions.java</a></p>
<p dir="auto">There, GraalVM is instructed to replace <code class="notranslate">oracle.jdbc.driver.OracleDriver.registerMBeans()</code> by a no-op method at native image runtime. However for the build analysis, this is ignored. <a class="user-mention notranslate" data-hovercard-type="user" data-hovercard-url="/users/zakkak/hovercard" data-octo-click="hovercard-link-click" data-octo-dimensions="link_type:self" href="https://github.com/zakkak">@zakkak</a> we have hit this several times already. Can you perhaps see any way forward inside GraalVM?</p>
<p dir="auto"><a class="user-mention notranslate" data-hovercard-type="user" data-hovercard-url="/users/dcdh/hovercard" data-octo-click="hovercard-link-click" data-octo-dimensions="link_type:self" href="https://github.com/dcdh">@dcdh</a> you may try to set <code class="notranslate">quarkus.native.additional-build-args = --initialize-at-run-time=oracle.jdbc.driver.OracleDriver</code> and see whether it will work for you. There is some info in</p>
<p dir="auto"><a href="https://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/f886e7bd7e061c52d78db014e5e97da15be26a64/extensions/jdbc/jdbc-oracle/deployment/src/main/java/io/quarkus/jdbc/oracle/deployment/OracleMetadataOverrides.java#L88-L92">https://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/f886e7bd7e061c52d78db014e5e97da15be26a64/extensions/jdbc/jdbc-oracle/deployment/src/main/java/io/quarkus/jdbc/oracle/deployment/OracleMetadataOverrides.java#L88-L92</a></p>
<p dir="auto">why Quarkus team won't adopt that option generally.</p></div></div><div role="toolbar" aria-label="Reactions" class="d-flex gap-1 flex-wrap"><div data-turbolinks="false"><div class="markdown-body safe-html-box__Uwc6Al Container__ZAxOBo"><blockquote>
<p dir="auto">does it mean it is safe to initialize the driver at runtime if we are using other drivers such as db2 <a href="https://github.com/ppalaga">@ppalaga</a> ?</p>
</blockquote>
<p dir="auto">The comment in <a href="https://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/f886e7bd7e061c52d78db014e5e97da15be26a64/extensions/jdbc/jdbc-oracle/deployment/src/main/java/io/quarkus/jdbc/oracle/deployment/OracleMetadataOverrides.java#L88-L92">https://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/f886e7bd7e061c52d78db014e5e97da15be26a64/extensions/jdbc/jdbc-oracle/deployment/src/main/java/io/quarkus/jdbc/oracle/deployment/OracleMetadataOverrides.java#L88-L92</a> suggests that it may not work. But it would still be interesting to see whether it really fails.</p></div></div><div role="toolbar" aria-label="Reactions" class="d-flex gap-1 flex-wrap"></div></div></div></div></div></div></div></li><li data-wrapper-timeline-id="IC_kwDOElz7Sc6eCL3Y"><div class="timelineElement__l_1Q03 nonLeadingElement_inlineAvatars__g_btmU"><div aria-expanded="false" tabindex="0" class="prc-Button-ButtonBase-9n-Xk CommentActionsIconButton__rdmp8b prc-Button-IconButton-fyge7" data-loading="false" data-no-visuals="true" data-size="small" data-variant="invisible" aria-describedby="_R_v6kj92om3mr9mm5_" id="_R_16kj92om3mr9mm5_"><svg data-component="Octicon" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" class="octicon octicon-kebab-horizontal" viewBox="0 0 16 16" width="16" height="16" fill="currentColor" display="inline-block" overflow="visible" style="vertical-align:text-bottom"><path d="M8 9a1.5 1.5 0 1 0 0-3 1.5 1.5 0 0 0 0 3ZM1.5 9a1.5 1.5 0 1 0 0-3 1.5 1.5 0 0 0 0 3Zm13 0a1.5 1.5 0 1 0 0-3 1.5 1.5 0 0 0 0 3Z"></path></svg></button><span class="prc-TooltipV2-Tooltip-tLeuB" data-direction="n" data-component="Tooltip" role="tooltip" aria-hidden="true" id="_R_v6kj92om3mr9mm5_">More actions</span></div></div></div></div></div><div class="Body__scgEjp IssueCommentBody__DGWd7U"><div data-testid="markdown-body" data-team-hovercards-enabled="true" class="markdown-body" data-turbolinks="false"><div class="markdown-body safe-html-box__Uwc6Al Container__ZAxOBo"><p dir="auto"><a class="user-mention notranslate" data-hovercard-type="user" data-hovercard-url="/users/ppalaga/hovercard" data-octo-click="hovercard-link-click" data-octo-dimensions="link_type:self" href="https://github.com/ppalaga">@ppalaga</a> unfortunately I don't think substituting code that runs at build time is possible (as GraalVM uses the host JVM to run that initialization code at which point substitutions can't be applied -- there is no hotspot mechanism for substitutions).</p>
<p dir="auto">I see two ways to approach this:</p>
<ol dir="auto">
<li>Understand why/how <code class="notranslate">org.apache.cxf:cxf-rt-management</code> brings in these beans and avoid them at build time (e.g. removing some classes from the classpath or changing a property at build-time).</li>
<li>Detect the fields being initialized and reset their values using <code class="notranslate">@RecomputeFieldValue</code>. It looks like GraalVM already does this kind of work for the affected fields when JFR is enabled on the host, see <a href="https://github.com/oracle/graal/blob/master/substratevm/src/com.oracle.svm.core/src/com/oracle/svm/core/jfr/Target_javax_management_MBeanServerFactory.java">https://github.com/oracle/graal/blob/master/substratevm/src/com.oracle.svm.core/src/com/oracle/svm/core/jfr/Target_javax_management_MBeanServerFactory.java</a> I believe following the substitutions that are enabled when <a href="https://github.com/oracle/graal/blob/009a6f93e563afb1d110b42dc5951b3d6b4156f8/substratevm/src/com.oracle.svm.core/src/com/oracle/svm/core/jfr/HasJfrSupport.java#L62-L64">https://github.com/oracle/graal/blob/009a6f93e563afb1d110b42dc5951b3d6b4156f8/substratevm/src/com.oracle.svm.core/src/com/oracle/svm/core/jfr/HasJfrSupport.java#L62-L64</a> returns <code class="notranslate">true</code> one would be able to work-around this issue.</li>
</ol></div></div><div role="toolbar" aria-label="Reactions" class="d-flex gap-1 flex-wrap"></div></div></div></div></div></div></div></li><li data-wrapper-timeline-id="IC_kwDOElz7Sc6eDkRX"><div <p dir="auto">Understand why/how org.apache.cxf:cxf-rt-management brings in these beans</p>
</blockquote>
<p dir="auto">GraaVM complains about an MBean server in the image heap. When asked to track how it gets referenced, it names only a bunch of Oracle classes. Isn't the error message or the tracking misleading/incomplete? How it comes that adding cxf-rt-management triggers it, but there is no cxf-rt-management class involved in the report? Is there some other way to see how cxf-rt-management makes the MBean server appart in the image heap?</p>
<blockquote>
<p dir="auto">and avoid them at build time (e.g. removing some classes from the classpath</p>
</blockquote>
<p dir="auto">Which mechanism should I use for removing those classes? I am not aware of any Quarkus BuildItem that would allow for that. Isn't there anything better than BytecodeTransformerBuildItem that I could perhaps misuse for that?</p></div></div><div role="toolbar" aria-label="Reactions" class="d-flex gap-1 <p dir="auto">GraaVM complains about an MBean server in the image heap. When asked to track how it gets referenced, it names only a bunch of Oracle classes. <p dir="auto">The report showing how the MBean object was instantiated is:</p>

<p dir="auto">which indeed does not contain any reference to cxf (or relevant classes), however it uses <code class="notranslate">java.lang.management.ManagementFactory.getPlatformMBeanServer</code> which I intuitively assume that performs some kind of search based on some "registered", or available on the classpath mbeans. As a follow up I guess that there is something in <code class="notranslate">cxf-rt-management</code> (or another package pulled in as a dependency) that makes some MBean(s) reachable.</p>
<blockquote>
<p dir="auto">Is there some other way to see how cxf-rt-management makes the MBean server appart in the image heap?</p>
</blockquote>
<p dir="auto">Using a newer version of GraalVM (e.g. with <code class="notranslate">-Dquarkus.native.builder-image=quay.io/quarkus/ubi-quarkus-mandrel-builder-image:jdk-23</code>) you get a more detailed log that shows the object reachability graph:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="Object was reached by
